TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 17, 2012 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON  
The first order of business is to el ect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  
Past practice has been that the previous year’s president  conducts 
this part of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temp orary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT  
The acting chairperson declares nomi nations in order for the office of 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not 
be seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the 
nomination.)  Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT  
The newly elected president  declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY  
The president declares nominations in  order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER  
According to Board Policy, the trea surer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue. 
5

officiate the meeting until the election of a president has been completed.

WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into the hands 
of Mark S. Rajter, Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies belonging to 
or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or otherwise held 
according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by Resolution 
for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into the hands of said 
Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to be designated in such 
Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , is hereby 
directed to deposit all public monies, including tax monies now in or coming in to his 
hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the following depository banks: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Citizen’s Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be withdrawn 
by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , and in 
emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , or the 
Superintendent, Barbara A. Fowler, who may sign on the General Fund account and the 
Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belongin g to the District for the accounts 
established at PNC Bank for purposes of paying Troy Career Center expenses may be 
withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The Troy Career Center 
Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the account 
established at Bank of America for purposes of paying remaining health care claims may 
be withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of  the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, and for checks over $10,000, the additional handwritten signature of Jasen Witt, 
Assistant Superintendent for Human Resources  or Monica Papasian, Finance Director; 
and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the account 
established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and dependent care 
flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next Generation Enrollment, 
Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter or 
Finance Director, Monica Papasian, and 
13
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the District; 
and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested according to 
Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine money from 
more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment authorized by subsection 
(1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School Code; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the funds of the District may be invested with the 
following institutions: 
Ambassador Capital Management 
Bank of America 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Charter One Bank 
Citizens Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
The Private Bank 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is regulated 
by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting thereof 
held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of Oakland in 
the State of Michigan this 17th day of January 2012. 
_______________________________ 
Secretary, Troy Board of Education

2. C.    01.17.12 
2. RECOGNITION 
C. 2011 Siemens Competition Winners  
 Three Troy High School teens will share $50,000 in scholarship money after 
coming in second place in early December in the nation’s premier science 
research competition.  
 Seniors, Edgar Wang and Justin Yuan, along with junior , Wayne Shu, were the 
only Michigan students competing in a field of 20 in the national finals of the 
Siemens Competition, a signature program of the Siemens Foundation which 
supports science, technology, engin eering and mathematics education, held at 
George Washington University in Washington D.C.  
 Would these three students please come forward at this time ?  
Recommended Action:  Distribute Certificates 
12
      2. D.   01.17.12 
2.  RECOGNITION       
D.    Board of Education Recognition  
Each January the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
(play video) 
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who are 
tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the 
opportunities of our students. The successes that we receive in the 
Troy School District stem in large part from the support and guidance 
we receive from our Board member s. The Troy Board of Education 
works very hard to e nsure that the entire district is committed to 
promoting student achievement . Tonight, we would like to recognize 
Board of Education  members with a gift both meaningful and 
heartfelt. One of our esteemed art teachers, Heather Kelly, who was 
honored at Art Prize this year, has created seven fabulous canvas 
pieces that are meant to symbolize our appreciation for your service 
to the District and t he wider Troy Community. Ms. Kelly graciously 
donated her time and talents for this endeavor, which truly 
encapsulates how lucky we are to have such a tremendous staff of 
professionals working with our students each and every day, and 
such a wonderful Board behind us.  
Thank you for all you do.

BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING     MINUTES   DECEMBER 6, 2011 
       SERVICES BUILDING  
  President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7:00 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Ms.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Malven, Miletti, and 
Underwood.   Also present were Superintendent Fowler; 
administrators  Keeling,  Machesky,  Rajter,  and  Witt  and 
approximately 45 additional members of the audience.   
RECOGNITION 
InvenTeamTM Grant Award 
The Board recognized Troy High School physics teacher, 
John  Morrison,  and  physics  club  students  who  won 
InvenTeam in this year's prestigious Lemelson‐MIT STEM 
competition  for  their  innovative  mechanical‐assist  stair 
climber  project.  The  Troy  High  School  team;  Sandeep 
Chen, Matthew Cui, Rolland He, Lawrence Kim, Maxwell 
Li,  Richard  Mu,  Nikhil  Ramu,  Phillip  Yang,  Clark  Zhang, 
and  Arnold  Zhou  are  just  one  of  15  high  school  teams 
across the country to be awarded $10,000 to develop a 
prototype  of  their  project.  In  June,  the  students  will 
showcase  their  stair  climber  at  EurekaFest  on  the  MIT 
campus in Cambridge, Massachusetts.   
Board Member Recognition ‐ 
M’Liz Malven 
M’Liz Malven has served as trustee of the Troy Board of 
Education  since  February  of  2011.  The  Board  honored 
Ms. Malven for her service and continued commitment 
to  the  students  of  the  Troy  School  District.   M’Liz 
volunteered to fill the vacancy on the Board last winter.  
During  her  tenure  on  the  Board,  M’Liz  helped  to  shape 
policy  to  comply  with  numerous  new  state  and  federal 
mandates.  Though  State  funding  was  reduced  in  the 
time  M’Liz  served  on  the  Board,  she  strove  to  put 
students  first  and  pushed  for  reductions  that  had  the 
least  impact  on  student  programming.   M’Liz  has  been 
an  active  parent  volunteer  in  the  Troy  school  and 
community organizations and initiatives. She served as a 
PTO Officer in both elementary and middle schools and 
15
Arakali,  David
  Bai,  Nitin  Chandra,  Allen  Chen,  Kathy 
was President of the PTO Council and Vice President of 
SAFE HOMES, Troy Community Coalition, and Troy Youth 
Assistance.  The  Board  thanked  Ms.  Malven  for  her 
exemplary  service  on  the  Board  and  for  her  dedication 
to  making  the  Troy  Community  a  better  place  for 
children.  They presented her with flowers.   
Ms.  Malven  thanked  the  Board  and  the  community  for 
the opportunity to serve and said that she will always be 
around  to  lend  her  support  and  looks  forward  to 
participating in Finance 101 classes if offered again.

CURRICULUM 
consideration of a full‐day kindergarten, which was due 
to  pending  legislation  that  would  change  the  State’s 
funding of kindergarten students.  She indicated that for 
receive  half  of  the  funding  for  half‐day  kindergarten 
students.   Dr.  Fowler  stated  that  Jan  Keeling,  Assistant 
Superintendent  of  elementary  instruction,  initiated 
meetings  to  determine  the  actions  needed  to  prepare 
for a full‐day kindergarten program.  She stated that the 
District  would  see  over  $3  million  loss  in  revenue  from 
the State if we continued a half‐day program and that in 
contrast  it  would  cost  approximately  $1.6  million  to 
implement a full‐day program.   She stated that the loss 
in revenue vs. the increase in cost to the District and the 
positive  impact  all‐day  instruction  would  have  on 
students; the decision was made to implement the full‐
day program.  She added that the cost for the additional 
kindergarten  rooms,  furniture,  materials  and  staffing 
would be included in the June budget for approval.     
Dr.  Fowler  then  introduced  Dr.  Joan  Lessen‐Firestone, 
showed  a  presentation,  “Full‐Day  Kindergarten:  Why 
Does  it  Matter?”  to  the  Board.   She  discussed  the 
benefits  all‐day  kindergarten  has  on  children.   She 
reviewed the overall findings of research that had been 
conducted  with  high‐quality  studies.   She  said  that  all 
children,  especially  at‐risk  children  benefit  from

Director  of  Early  Childhood  at  Oakland

developmentally‐appropriate  curriculum,  most  notably 
in  academic  achievement.   She  added  that  gains  are

studies  have  shown  a  dramatic  increase  in  academic 
retention is also prevented for students who attend all‐
day  programs.   She  added  that  ELL  children  are  very 
successful in all‐day programs and that there are faster 
gains on literacy and language measures.   She said that 
gains  are  also  seen  in  the  area  of  math  for  children 
above poverty level.   She added that children in full‐day 
programs tend to obey rules and work well with others 
and  that  parents  and  teachers  cite  improved  school 
socialization,  less  stress  and  frustration.   She  indicated 
that  there  is  more  time  for  individual  and  small‐group 
instruction  and  less  whole  group,  which  benefits  both 
struggling  students  as  well  as  the  more  advanced 
students.   She  stated  that  the  second  half  of  an  all‐day 
kindergarten  program  is  very  productive  and  that 
students  are  engaged,  there  is  a  lot  of  hands‐on  and 
active learning and the fact that full‐day kindergartners 
are  no  more  fatigued  than  those  attending  half‐day 
programs  and  that  statistics  show  better  attendance 
with  full‐day  kindergartners.   She  concluded  her 
presentation stating that full‐day kindergarten will make 
a  difference  for  students  in  the  Common  Core 
Standards.

Mr.  Kumar  Bhatt  addressed  the  Board  requesting  that 
the  Public  Communication  for  agenda  items  and  non‐
asked if Troy School District being the policyholder was 
the  same  as  being  self‐insured.   He  also  congratulated 
Dr.   Machesky  on  the  success  of  his  summer  program 
Union  and  Athens  since  many  of  them  have  lower 
academic  achievers  and  hopes  the  program  is 
implemented at all grade levels.   
Ms.  Philippart  responded  to  Mr.  Bhatt  regarding  the 
distinction  between  Public  Communication  being  split 
between  agenda  and  non‐agenda  items.   She  indicated 
Public Communication was recently split into two items 
due to the large volume of speakers they experienced in

6. A.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Appointment of Board Negotiating Team for  
                                 TESPA Contract Negotiations

the TESPA/MEA/NEA ex pires on June 30, 20 12.  This union represents the

7. A. CURRICULUM 
REPORT: Oakland Community Accelerated Learning Program 
(OCALP)  
This past summer several Troy School District  students participated in a 
summer learning opportunity hosted by Oakland Community College . OCALP 
is a program designed to give students from five partner district s an 
opportunity to expose students to science and technology courses  while 
earning college credits.

7. B.    01.17.12 
7. CURRICULUM 
B. T.R.O.Y. Elementary Fitness Program  
The Elementary Physical Education Department is committed to instilling 
in all students the skills, fitness and positive character traits that lead to a 
healthy, active lifestyle -for life.  They are proud to share with you a new 
fitness program they have d eveloped called T.R.O.Y. (Teachers 
Recognizing Outstanding Youth) Fitness Program.  This program focuses 
on students challenging themselves to continually increase their personal 
fitness level rather than comparing themselves to others.  This new 
program w ill replace the Presidential Physical Fitness Award in which 
fourth and fifth grade students have traditionally participated.  The 
physical education teachers have found that the Presidential Physical 
Fitness Award only motivated a small percentage of thei r students and 
feel the T.R.O.Y Fitness Program will motivate all students.   
Recommended Action:   
Jamie Grant, physical education teacher, will present this evening’s report.

7. CURRICULUM 
C. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice, Section 105 - Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts are required to pass 
a resolution each school year indicating whether or not they plan to 
implement a Schools of Choice program for nonresident students who 
attend another district within Oakland County.  I t would be appropriate at 
this time for the Board of Education to consider adopting a Schools of 
Choice, Section 105 resolution for nonresident students who attend a school 
district within Oakland County .  We would advertise for 100 kindergarten 
and 30 first grade nonresident students.  This would include both Oakland 
County and non-Oakland County residents. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School District, 
exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School Aid Act of 1979, as 
amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept application s for a li mited Schools of 
Choice program  for the 2012 -2013 school year for nonresident kindergarten and 
nonresident first grade students who attend a school district within Oakland County. 
Ayes: __________ 
Nays: __________

D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice, Section 105c - Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts are required to pass 
a resolution each school year indicating whether or not they plan to 
implement a Schools of Choice program for nonresident students.   It would 
be appropriate at this time for the Board of Education to consider adopting a 
Schools of Choice , Section 105 c resolution for nonresident students who 
attend a school district in another county, if that Intermediate School District 
is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.  We 
would advertise for 100 kindergarten and 30 first grade nonresident 
students.  This would include both Oakland County and non-Oakland County 
residents. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution.

amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a li mited Schools of 
Choice program  for the 2012 -2013 school year for nonresident kindergarten and 
nonresident first grade students who attend a school district in another county, if that 
Intermediate School District is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District 
boundaries.

E.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice, Section 105 – Baker Middle School 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution each 
school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It is anticipated 
that seats will be available for the 2012 -2013 school year for grades 6 - 8 at Baker 
Middle School.  It would be appropriate at this time to consider adopting a Schools of 
Choice, Section 105 resolution for nonresident 6th – 8th grade students who attend a 
school district within Oakland C ounty. We would advertise for 25 nonresident 6 th – 8th 
grade students who attend a school district within Oakland County.  This would include 
both Oakland County and non-Oakland County residents.

SUGGESTED RESOLUTION

amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools of 
Choice program for the 2012-2013 school year for nonresident students who attend a 
school district within Oakland County for the purposes of attending grades 6 – 8 at 
Baker Middle School.
